- [x] **I5** Sign and install IPAs with your own certificate, on the website (user 2026-11-17: 'do we have our own way to sign and load ipas using a mobile provision cert? we should'). Cydian's own signer, not another project's. Settings > Signing: the user picks their .p12, its password and a .mobileprovision; the page shows the certificate name, team, expiry and how many devices the profile lists, and keeps the files in IndexedDB on that phone only. On an AltStore app's page (F29), Install becomes Sign & Install when a certificate is set: the browser uploads the IPA URL plus the cert, profile and password to POST /sign over HTTPS; the server (web/app.py, a new web/signer/) downloads the IPA (size cap, http/https only, no private addresses: SSRF), signs it with zsign (check its licence fits GPL-3.0-or-later and record it; built into the Docker image) using the profile's entitlements and bundle ID, holds the signed IPA under a random unguessable token for 10 minutes, and answers with an itms-services:// link whose manifest and IPA it serves from that token; the page opens it. Privacy, stated on the page: the certificate, key and password are used in memory for that one request and never written to disk or logs; signed IPAs are deleted after 10 minutes or first download. Rate-limit per address. Clear errors for a wrong password, an expired cert or profile, or a profile whose app ID doesn't fit. Write docs/signing.md (how it works, what is stored where, limits). t: Python tests with a throwaway self-signed cert and a tiny fixture IPA (signature verifies with the profile's team; token expires; SSRF and size checks refuse); node tests for the settings parsing (cert name, expiry, device count from the profile plist); the user signs a real app on their iPhone and reports. | files: web/app.py, web/signer/**, web/Dockerfile, docker-compose.yml, web/app/screens/settings.js, web/app/core/signing.js, docs/signing.md | after: F29 — User 2026-11-17: 'users should be able to have their own server, or do it all local so they dont rely on me'. So Settings > Signing has a Signing Server field: cydian.dev by default, or any other server's address. Anyone can run the signer on their own machine (docker compose up from this repo; document it in docs/signing.md and the README: one command, which port, HTTPS needed for itms-services e.g. behind Caddy or Tailscale serve). The page talks to whichever server is set (CORS for /sign; nothing tied to cydian.dev). The fully local path, signing on the phone with no server at all, is I6. — User 2026-11-17: with our own signing, Install sits in the nav bar's top right like Cydia's package page (F29 puts Install/Modify there): when a certificate is set, that button signs and installs (label stays Install, as Cydia; a HUD 'Signing' then 'Installing'), not a row in the page. (2) The walkthrough says plainly which steps installing needs: with a Signing Server set (cydian.dev by default) only the certificate is needed, since 0.1.4's installs take the manifest from the server and the IPA over plain HTTP from 127.0.0.1 (Sideload.swift manifestServer), so the local-trust steps (download, install and trust Cydian's profile) are shown as optional ('Only to install with no server at all'), collapsed; with no server set they're required. signingReady/the Install button must not wait for trust when a server is set. The app makes a random 256-bit key, opens Safari at <server>/#/settings/signing/export with the key in the URL fragment (never sent to a server); that page reads the identity from that origin's IndexedDB (signing-client loadIdentity), encrypts it with AES-GCM under the key (WebCrypto) and opens cydian://signing-import#<base64 ciphertext+iv>; the app decrypts and imports to the Keychain (signing.import), then shows the walkthrough ticked. Only ciphertext ever travels in a URL, so another app claiming cydian:// gets nothing usable. If that origin has no certificate (Home Screen web apps and Safari keep separate storage, and cydian.dev and the test site are separate origins), the page says so and offers to choose the files there. - [x] **F163** Swipe back fades the row highlight with the gesture (native app) (user 2026-11-18: "when swiping back in native app the current menu stays highlighted until swipe is complete when leaving, it shouldnt. the original in 4s screenshot 77 shows how the original has a sort of fade out as it gets closer to being back. we need that"). During the interactive edge-swipe back, the row you opened, on the page being revealed, stays fully blue until the swipe ends, then clears at once. Instead, tie its highlight opacity to the swipe progress: fully blue at the start, faded out by the time the page is back, as UIKit does (deselectRowAtIndexPath animated / transitionCoordinator). A cancelled swipe brings it back to full. Tapping Back fades it over the pop animation in the same way. Opacity only, at 120 Hz. Note: the user's "4S screenshot 77" was not found; /var/mobile/Media/DCIM/*/IMG_0077.PNG on the 4S is an f.lux settings page, so ask the user for the right shot (or record the gesture with tools/ui-4s.sh under its lock). Test on the iPhone 7 jailbreak build (tools/ui-7.sh). t: node test for highlight opacity as a function of swipe progress, including the cancelled case.
- [x] **F166** Spinners don't spin in the jailbreak app (user 2026-11-18: "the jailbreak app spinners dont spin right now"). The HUD, reload-bar, reloading-bar-button and list spinners all use `@keyframes hud-spin` (ios6.css ~281-287, home.css ~115, packages.css ~54, ios6.css ~471, the reload bar ~537) with `steps(12)` and stand still in the native WKWebView app. Find out why on the iPhone 7 jailbreak build (ssh 7, tools/ui-7.sh; Safari Web Inspector over USB if it helps). Candidates: the native shell pausing CSS animations or rAF, a `html.native` rule overriding `animation`, the spinner element being replaced every frame (restarting at frame 0), steps(12) on a composited layer, or Reduce Motion. Fix it so every spinner turns at 12 steps per 0.96 s in the app, as on the website, and check the sideload IPA build too. t: node/CSS test that no native-only rule disables the spinner animation, plus a note in journal/ with the cause and a screen recording or two screenshots showing different frames.
- [x] **F160** Compatibility for apps whose source has no iOS range (user 2026-11-18: "lots of apptesters repos and others have a ? do we just not import right or they have no field for it"). Checked: AppTesters (10,304 apps) has no minOSVersion/maxOSVersion at all; Quantum 4/38, WuXu 18/35. The IPA itself has it: tools/ipa-plist-probe.py reads Payload/*.app/Info.plist's MinimumOSVersion and UIDeviceFamily with HTTP Range requests (zip end record or zip64, central directory, the one entry; 100-350 KB per app, GitHub release assets answer 206). 1) Worker route GET /api/ipainfo?url=<https IPA url> (worker/src/index.js, same allow-list and limits as /api/fetch, follow redirects, Range requests, inflate-raw with DecompressionStream, binary or XML plist parsing): answers {minOSVersion, deviceFamily, bundleId, version} or {error}; cached at the edge for a long time (keyed by URL) so each IPA is read once for everyone; also in worker/node.mjs for self-hosters. 2) The app/website asks only for apps whose version has no minOSVersion, lazily (rows on screen, the app page), a few at a time, stores results on the device keyed by downloadURL, and feeds them to core/compat.js / altstore.js runsOn as if the source had them (source fields win). 3) UIDeviceFamily without 1 (iPad-only) marks the app not for iPhone on an iPhone. 4) Fallback when Range fails: parse 'iOS 15+' / 'Requires iOS 14.0' style text in the description or subtitle. Native apps may read the IPA directly instead of the Worker. t: worker test with a small fixture zip (and a zip64 one) served with Range; node tests for the merge into compat and the lazy queue; tools/check.sh green. | files: worker/src/index.js, worker/node.mjs, worker/test.mjs, web/app/core/compat.js, web/app/core/altstore.js, web/app/core/ipainfo.js — 2026-11-18 user: check only when an app's page is opened, never for lists/feeds. Lists show a new 'unchecked' mark (grey outlined ?, distinct from today's ?) for apps with no source range and no saved result; opening the app page asks /api/ipainfo once, the page shows the result, and it's saved on the device so the row shows green/red afterwards. Today's ? then means checked but unknown (Range refused, no Info.plist). Combine with F161's App Store lookup (the store's minimumOsVersion is a fallback when the IPA can't be read; the IPA's own value wins).

- [x] **B21** Signing fails on IPAs with an executable-less bundle: "YTUHD.bundle/YTUHD is missing." installing YouMod (user 2026-11-18 screenshot, sideload iFizBox: Downloading, Reading, Signing, then Couldn't Install YouMod). Modded IPAs inject tweak bundles whose Info.plist names a CFBundleExecutable that isn't there. zsign and Feather treat such a .bundle as resources (seal its files in CodeResources, no binary to sign) and carry on; ours stops. Fix it in every signer we run (native/sign + the zsign WASM path used by the website, wherever 'is missing' is raised): a missing CFBundleExecutable in a nested .bundle (and .framework without a binary) is skipped with a log line in the install log, not an error; the main app's own executable missing stays an error. Test with a small fixture IPA that has an empty .bundle naming a missing executable; then on the iPhone 7 / sideload build if possible with YouMod from an AppTesters-style source. | files: native/sign/**, native/Sources/SignBridge.swift, web/app/vendor/zsign/**, tools/zsign-wasm/**, web/fixtures/** | test: a signer test with the executable-less bundle fixture; tools/check.sh

- [x] **F190** Website install: show what iOS is doing after 'Installing' (user 2026-11-18: 'the last log is still installing. can we have better logs'). Safari never hears back from itms-services, but the Worker sees iOS's own requests (worker tail of a working install: appstored GETs manifest.plist, HEAD app.ipa, then GET app.ipa once /done lands). Record those per token in the upload's KV metadata (manifest fetched, download started, download finished: the body fully streamed) with at most ~3 extra writes per install (free tier 1,000 writes/day), and add GET /sign/up/<token>/status -> { manifest, started, finished }. signInBrowser polls it (every 1.5 s, up to EARLY_WINDOW) and the HUD/log steps through: 'Waiting for iOS…' (until manifest), 'iOS is downloading iFizBox…', 'Downloaded. iOS is installing it: watch the Home Screen.' then done; if iOS never asks within ~60 s: 'iOS didn't ask for the app. Tap Install again.' with a Retry that opens the itms link. Show each step for at least ~0.4 s so a fast install still reads as steps, not a flash.

- [x] **B53** Tap during momentum scroll: on iOS 6, a tap while a list is still coasting from a flick only stops the scroll; it does not select the row or press the button under the finger. The next tap does. iFizBox (site, Home Screen web app, and the native WKWebView app) currently treats that first tap as a real tap. Fix: one global guard in web/app/app.js: if the scroll container under the finger was still moving at touchstart/pointerdown (scroll position changed in the last ~100 ms, or the scroll has velocity), swallow the matching click (capture phase, preventDefault + stopPropagation) and skip the row's pressed highlight. Then cover the other handlers that act on pointerdown/touchstart (nav/rowswipe.js, nav/sectionindex.js, screens/*.js onPress) so they also ignore that touch. Taps on a still list must stay instant. Check: a node test for the guard logic (moving: swallowed; still: passes); on the iPhone 7 (ui-7.sh under its lock), flick Changes or a long section, tap a row mid-coast: the scroll stops and no package opens; a second tap opens it. Compare against Cydia on the 4S (ui-4s.sh lock) and note the timing in the journal. | files: web/app/app.js, web/app/app.test.js, web/app/nav/rowswipe.js, web/app/nav/sectionindex.js — user chose: remove F153 (iOS 6 behaviour) — dropped: replaced by a task with the right files (remove F153)

- [x] **F230** (follow-up, critical) Theme > Light / Dark preview split (F215, screens/settings.js splitPane/layoutSplit/dragSplit), user 2026-11-18: (1) Where the user drags the divider lasts only for this visit: leaving the page (Back, another tab) and coming back starts fresh. Drop the localStorage position (SPLIT_KEY, savedSplit/saveSplit): keep it in the in-memory split state, which resetSplit() already clears when a theme list opens (settings.js ~L100); remove the old stored key once (storage.removeItem) so nobody keeps a stale one. Within the visit a drag still wins over the tap size, across the redraw a theme tap causes. (2) Start lower: on entry the preview is a short peek, its divider plus the sample's nav bar (about 100px, at least SPLIT_MIN, never covering the list's rows: snap to a row edge as entrySplit does), instead of a quarter of the screen. (3) Pop in when a theme is chosen: the first theme tap of the visit (no drag yet) springs the preview up to half the screen (SPLIT_TAP) with a small overshoot and settle, about 350-400ms, transform only (120 Hz, docs/native.md); later taps keep the height. Reduced motion: no spring, jump. (6) The sample nav bar's Edit (top right) is misaligned: compare its size, right inset and vertical centre with Back and with a real nav bar's right button (ios6.css .bar-button, e.g. Sources' Edit) in a 3x render zoomed in, light and dark, and match them. — More from the user 2026-11-18: (7) The divider grip: draw it in the style of iOS 6's Notification Center grabber, flipped. - [x] **B64** (follow-up, critical) Sideload: other apps went through the server's /sign/host, which is only meant for iFizBox's own update (user 2026-11-18, 0.9.8: an update failed with 'server: This server hosts only iFizBox's own signed app' while signing with a * wildcard profile, so iFizBox's own ID was com.ryha.ifizbox and allowed: the refused upload was another app). Likely cause: Sideload.hostServer is one static var (native/Sources/Sideload.swift ~L137, set at L151 by each signing.install and L521 by each resign call, read at L306 after signing finishes on SignBridge.queue). When the queue starts the next job while the previous one is still signing (queue.js passes host only for iFizBox's own job, ~L427/L448, and 'after' lets jobs overlap), iFizBox's call sets hostServer and an app signed earlier then reads it and uploads to /sign/host. Fix: decide hosting per call (capture the host server in the job/closure; never a shared static), likewise manifestServer if it has the same problem; MyIPAs.swift L219 too. Other apps must always install from the phone's own 127.0.0.1 server (the host also caps uploads at 25 MB). The Worker now accepts any app on /sign/host (HOST_IDS '*', so the symptom is gone for small apps), but big apps would still fail there. Check: a test for the per-call host choice if the Swift side has a test path (else a JS-side test that only iFizBox's own job sends host: true, and the journal reasoning for the Swift change); native/build.sh builds both; tools/check.sh. If you can, reproduce on the iPhone 7 is not possible (jailbreak build refuses signing.*): say so. | files: native/Sources/Sideload.swift, native/Sources/MyIPAs.swift, web/app/screens/queue.js, web/app/screens/*.test.js

Fix: labels carry the percent separately (or hudLine pulls a percent from anywhere), so the log gets one line per phase per app and the percent only moves the bar and status line; check every h.set caller in signing-client.js / signing-sheet.js for the same. (no push: wildcards can't have it); (5) extensions: left out, or kept under the same application-identifier? Needs the user's Ad Hoc profile with Push: ask them for it in the journal if missing. - [x] **B71** iPhone 7 jailbreak .deb 0.9.8-1605: swiping a row left doesn't queue it (user 2026-11-19), though the build has F193's attachQueueSwipe (screens/browse.js packageList, used by Changes too). Suspects: the swipe gives AltStore app (kind 'ipa') rows nothing in the jailbreak build by design (queueKind 'apt'), so app upgrades can't be swiped (they should queue the way the jailbreak build installs apps); touch handling on iOS 15 WKWebView (scroller steals the pan, touch-action); queueOf() null at attach time. Fix so a left swipe queues Install/Upgrade/Remove/Cancel on every queueable row in the jailbreak build. Check: a test that fails before the fix, tools/check.sh, then a real swipe on the 7. | files: web/app/screens/browse.js, web/app/screens/changes.js, web/app/screens/*.test.js | after: B69
- [x] **F237** Keep the app's own bundle ID when signing, on by default (user 2026-11-19), so a signed app doesn't duplicate the original and, with an Ad Hoc profile that has Push, gets its notifications. Build on R25's verdict (exact entitlements, extensions, what it found about two apps and the App Store copy). Both signers (native/sign IPASigner + Profile.bundleID, and the website's core/websign.js targetBundleId/appEntitlements + zsign call): Info.plist keeps CFBundleIdentifier; application-identifier and keychain group come from the profile's App ID (explicit, or the wildcard narrowed). The old way stays as an option: a global setting in Settings > Signing (by the certificate), something like 'Use My Bundle ID' / 'Don't Duplicate Bundle IDs', and the same switch per app in Custom Install. Choosing the old way warns plainly: the app becomes a separate copy under your ID, its notifications won't work, sign-in/iCloud/shared data tied to its ID may not. When the certificate or profile can't do the new way well (development profile: sandbox push only; no Push / aps-environment production; a wildcard profile, which Apple won't give Push), say so before signing (it still keeps the ID if R25 says that installs; else falls back to your ID and says that) and link a help page: a new guide (screens/guides.js) with R25's steps made simple: make an explicit App ID with Push on, register your iPhones' UDIDs, make an Ad Hoc profile for it, import it in Settings > Signing. Warn too when the original app (same ID) is installed and would be replaced. Check: node tests for the ID/entitlement choice per profile kind and option, the warnings and the guide link; native/sign tests for the kept ID; on the iPhone 7 sideload build an app signed both ways installs. | files: native/sign/IPASigner.swift, native/sign/Profile.swift, native/sign/SignOptions.swift, native/sign/tests/**, web/app/core/websign.js, web/app/core/signing.js, web/app/screens/signing.js, web/app/screens/guides.js, web/app/core/appqueue.js, web/app/settings.js | after: R25 — R25 done (journal/2026-11-18-r25-keep-bundle-id: exact entitlements, extensions keep their own IDs under the app's application-identifier, code identifier = Info.plist ID so no zsign -b). Warn when one explicit App ID would be shared by several apps (shared keychain group: sign-ins mix), and when the App Store copy is installed (iOS refuses; deleting it deletes its data). The user tests push with an Arctic Sign certificate (Push on) on their iPhone 15 Pro once this ships; whatever its profile's App ID (explicit, '*', or prefix.*), keep-ID must work with it.
- [x] **B72** iPhone 7 jailbreak .deb: installing YouMod (an AltStore app) failed with 'iOS didn't install' / installd refused the app (user 2026-11-19). Find installd's real reason: re-run the helper's install-ipa on the same IPA over ssh 7 with sudo -n (and/or sudo oslog), not by tapping Install. Likely causes: MinimumOSVersion above 15.8 (current YouTube needs a newer iOS), app extensions/plugins with entitlements AppSync's fakesign doesn't cover (helper only fakesigns when the main binary lacks application-identifier; extensions need it too), or a bundle ID clash. Fix what's ours (fakesign every Mach-O in PlugIns/Extensions/Frameworks; check MinimumOSVersion before downloading and say so on the page and the app's install row), and show installd's own error text in the console/alert instead of a generic failure. Write the finding to journal/<ID>/notes.md and docs/iphone7.md. Uninstall anything test-installed afterwards. Check: tools/check.sh plus a helper test for the new fakesign/version check, then the YouMod install on the 7 succeeds or shows the precise reason. | files: native/helper/**, web/app/screens/ipa-install.js, docs/iphone7.md

All builds: jailbreak app, Lite and the website. Check: node --test that a one-item run opens on Log and a two-item run on the list; tools/check.sh passes. | files: web/app/nav/progress.js, web/app/screens/apprun.js, web/app/screens/queuerun.js
- [x] **B79** (follow-up, critical) Sideload IPA: the app queue stops taking apps. User 2026-11-19: in the IPA, Installed > an app > Reinstall, then Continue Queuing, then Reinstall (another app) adds nothing to the queue. Path: altstore.js appPageAction -> queue.js queueApp -> core/appqueue.js add -> openAppConfirm; Continue Queuing is continueGroup(close). Suspects: (1) queueApp only opens Confirm when appRunning is false, and appRunning is set back only by runAppQueueRun's onClose/reopenConfirm (apprun.js page.onClose). Any path that leaves it true (a run page closed some other way, ready() throwing, F247's single-item Log page) makes every later add land silently with no sheet. (2) appConfirm still pointing at a closed sheet, so openAppConfirm calls refresh() on a dead modal. (3) the job is dropped because add() replaces by id, or appJob returns null for Reinstall. Repro it in node --test first (queue two Reinstalls with Continue Queuing in between, also after a finished or cancelled run), fix the cause, and keep the test. - [x] **B80** My IPAs: an IPA that shares a bundle ID with the app on the phone shows Installed even though that IPA was never installed. Example: 'RyukGram No Plugins' and 'RyukGram' both use the same ID, only RyukGram is installed, and both rows say Installed. Cause: installedOf() (web/app/screens/myipas.js) keys the installed map only by bundle ID (apps.isInstalled + Installed's records). Fix: when several IPAs in the library share an installed ID, mark only the one that matches what's on the phone as Installed. Match on Installed's record for that ID (recordInstall keeps name/version/key; the file/key that was installed is the best match, then name+version). The rows that don't match show Not Installed, with a small subtitle such as 'Replaces <installed app name>' (iOS 6 grouped-row subtitle style; no new controls). Installing or queueing one of those rows (libraryJob/queueLibraryInstall, mode 'reinstall' today) must warn first with an iOS 6 alert: '<installed name> is already on this iPhone with the same ID. Installing <this name> will replace it.' with Cancel and Replace. With only one IPA for an ID, keep today's behaviour. If there's no record to tell them apart (e.g. installed outside iFizBox), mark none Installed and show the replace subtitle and warning on all of them. Check: node --test for myipas-page/myipas-queue tests with two entries sharing an ID (only the recorded one Installed, the other gets the subtitle and its install warns); tools/check.sh passes. | files: web/app/screens/myipas.js, web/app/screens/myipas*.test.js, web/app/core/installed.js

- [x] **B76** Lite (sideload IPA): installing an app and leaving the app (or the screen) ends with 'iOS didn't install it' (core/apprun.js startInstall when signing.progress answers installed:false; native/Sources/Sideload.swift progress/watch). On a stock phone iFizBox can't watch iOS's install once it is in the background, so a false there means 'lost sight', not 'refused'. Fix: once iOS has the IPA (the install server's onServed, 'iOS has the whole IPA') or the app went to the background during the watch, treat it as handed off to iOS: the row ends Done ('Sent <app> to iOS', unsure: true, as installed:null already does) and leaves the queue, never Failed. Keep Failed only for a real refusal we can see (iOS never fetched the IPA while we stayed in front, or an install error line). Check with signing.isInstalled when coming back to the foreground and upgrade the row to Installed if iOS has it. - [x] **B83** (follow-up, critical) Sideload self-update often doesn't land (user 2026-11-19, 0.9.8 -> 0.9.9 on their phone: 1st and 2nd Upgrade showed the progress page and the 'iFizBox Updated' notification, but iFizBox opened still on the old version; the 3rd worked; another app was updating at the same time). Cause (read from the code, not yet seen on a device): CloseForUpdate (native/Sources/Sideload.swift) posts 'iFizBox Updated / Tap to open the new version' on an 8 s timer as soon as iOS's install prompt is answered, then exit(0); nothing checks that iOS fetched /sign/host/<t>/app.ipa or finished installing. With a slow download (another install in iOS's queue, a 25 MB IPA from KV) the user taps the notification first and opens the OLD app, which iOS won't install over while it runs (the 2026-11-18 finding behind CloseForUpdate), so the update is lost or stalls. Second suspect: Workers KV is eventually consistent, so a get right after the put in /sign/host can 404 ('That install link has expired') and iOS gives up silently. Fix: (1) worker: /sign/host marks what iOS fetched, like /sign/up's F190 marks (manifest, started, finished) with GET /sign/host/<t>/status; app.ipa and manifest.plist retry the KV get for a few seconds before answering 404 (covers the KV lag). (2) native: after the prompt is answered, stay alive (the existing background audio keep-alive) and poll that status; only when app.ipa has streamed to its end post the notification (no fixed 8 s guess; wording that doesn't claim it's done before it is, e.g. 'Finishing the update…' until then) and exit; if iOS never fetches the manifest/IPA within ~2 min, don't exit: the progress page says the update didn't start and offers Try Again. (3) on launch, if a self-update to version X was recorded and app.info is still older, a banner says the update didn't finish (Try Again), instead of silence. Check: worker node tests for the marks and the KV retry; native build in the gate; on a sideload install (not the iPhone 7 jailbreak app, see docs/iphone7.md; ask the user if no phone can run the Lite build) upgrade iFizBox while another app is installing and confirm it opens on the new version the first time. | files: native/Sources/Sideload.swift, worker/src/index.js, worker/test/**, web/app/signing-client.js, web/app/nav/progress.js, web/app/core/selfupdate.js, web/app/essential-ui.js

- [x] **B89** Lite on iOS 27: iOS can freeze the local install server's listening socket while iFizBox is in the background, so iOS never fetches the IPA and shows 'Unable to Install, please try again later'. Evidence (user's iPhone19,2, iOS 27.0.1, build 0.9.9-1676, 2026-11-19): serving began about 09:45:30, with KeepAlive's silent audio playing; no connection reached NWListener [L1] 127.0.0.1:52783 between 09:50 and 10:00; at 10:00:32, the 15-minute give-up, the cancel logged 'nw_listener_resume_all_inboxes_if_suspended … Resuming inbox socket: 18', so the socket had been suspended. symptomsd also logged 'CFSM dynamic denylist … com.ryha.ifizbox … do NOT allow' and 'COSMCtrl evictBackgroundApps: evicting com.ryha.ifizbox' at 09:51:48. The phone locked at 09:59:59. It doesn't happen every time: a later install fetched and installed fine. First find out when the socket gets suspended (screen locked? the app evicted? a long wait on the prompt?): capture idevicesyslog -m iFizBox -m installd over a whole failing attempt. Then fix it: resume or re-create the listener when it is suspended, or fall back to the Worker's /sign/host (hostAndOpen, already used for iFizBox's own update) when iOS hasn't connected within N seconds. Check: an install where the phone locks or iFizBox sits in the background still installs; native tests pass. | files: native/Sources/InstallServer.swift, native/Sources/Sideload.swift, native/Sources/InstallPrompt.swift — User 2026-11-19: queue the FIX, don't stop at research. Also resume or re-create the listener when iFizBox returns to the front. The syslog capture is still worth doing for the journal, but the fix must not wait on it.

journal/F181 journal/N6 journal/R1 | files: journal/2026-11-17-b1-dist-source-icon — Also (user 2026-11-19): anisette is required device data, not something to fake: use one stable anisette identity per user (anisette v3 style, client keeps its own provisioning), never random per sign-in (new device each time = 2FA every time). Name the certificate machine, device and App IDs plainly 'iFizBox' (from brand NAME), no disguising. Check which public anisette servers support v3. Plan the failure path: when Apple changes sign-in and it breaks, a clear error and a pointer to the certificate route (the recommended one). In the follow-up UI task: Home's certificate button subtitle is just 'Recommended · no weekly renew' (the longer text overflowed).
- [x] **B71a** Jailbreak build: queue AltStore apps from a row (swipe, hold menu) and from the app page's Modify, as tweaks queue: no install at once and no confirm popup on Remove (user 2026-11-19: 'it just adds to the queue'). Today app rows don't swipe in the jailbreak build (B71 checked on the iPhone 7: OpenTennis in Changes does nothing) because its only queue is apt's; the app queue (app-queue.js) signs, which the jailbreak build refuses. Needs: app jobs (Install/Upgrade/Remove) in a queue the jailbreak Confirm sheet and Q'd badge show, and a run that installs through the helper's install-ipa (ipa-install.js installWithAppSync) and removes through the Installed tab's helper uninstall (installed.js runDelete), quietly from a swipe with the undo bar (F193). Groundwork in browse.js: rowMenuItems({ jbApps, job, installed, newest }) and jailbreakAppRowState(item, { pkg, rec }) (tested in screens/rowswipe-jailbreak.test.js), not wired yet. The sideload (Lite) build's app-row swipe (F213 attachAppSwipe) must keep working: check it too. Check on the 7: swipe an app row in Changes and Search, Confirm runs it. | files: web/app/screens/browse.js, web/app/screens/queue.js, web/app/screens/apprun.js, web/app/core/appqueue.js, web/app/screens/*.test.js
- [x] **B92** Long IPA file names are cut off with no way to read them all (user 2026-11-19, Lite 0.9.9: opentennis-beta-p146.ipa ends in '…' and a tap doesn't show the rest). Do it the iOS 6 way: (1) file names truncate in the middle, so the start and the end stay visible ('opentennis-be…p146.ipa', like Mail attachments and Safari downloads; UILineBreakModeMiddleTruncation). CSS can't do that, so add one small JS helper that fits the name to the width, used by every row that shows an IPA or .deb file name (My IPAs, the install/progress page, the queue, Install IPAs). Keep tail truncation for app names and other text. (2) Hold a file-name row: the iOS 6 black Copy callout copies the full name, as Contacts does on a value row. If a tap opens the IPA's page, that page shows the full name wrapped, never cut. Check: node --test for the helper (short name unchanged; long name keeps the extension and the start; fits the given width), then a 3x render zoomed in on My IPAs with a long name, and tools/check.sh. | files: web/app/core/midtrunc.js, web/app/core/midtrunc.test.js, web/app/screens/myipas.js, web/app/myipas.css, web/app/progress.css, web/app/queue.css, web/app/screens/progress.js — concept approved
- [x] **B91** Lite: never open iOS's Install prompt again by itself. B89's freeze fallback (Sideload.swift hostAfterFreeze) uploads the IPA to /sign/host and then calls InstallPrompt.open(hosted…), so a new 'Install?' pops up unasked. The user may have tapped Cancel, and iFizBox can't tell a cancelled prompt from a frozen server. Keep the upload, but don't open the prompt: the row ends Cancelled, as B85's dismissed path does ('Cancelled on the iOS prompt'), and offers Install again. Only the user's tap (the row's re-prompt, signing.reprompt) opens the prompt, using the hosted link when the local listener froze, or else the local one. Look at InstallPrompt's own automatic re-opens (checkShown, and the B85 'retried when iOS drops it' path) too: re-opening when no prompt ever appeared is fine, re-opening after a prompt was shown and closed is not. Check: native tests pass with a test that a freeze after the prompt closed opens no new prompt; on the phone, Cancel on the prompt gives a Cancelled row with no new prompt, and Install again installs, also after the phone locked. | files: native/Sources/Sideload.swift, native/Sources/InstallPrompt.swift, native/test_install_server.py, web/app/core/apprun.js, web/app/screens/apprun.js — User 2026-11-19, replaces 'ends Cancelled': iFizBox can't tell the user's Cancel from a frozen server, so don't call it Cancelled. The row ends Failed with a generic line, for example 'iOS didn't download it', and the retry (Try Again) is open on that row. The tap opens the prompt again, as above (the hosted link when the listener froze). Keep 'Cancelled' only for what iFizBox knows: the user's own Stop or Cancel in iFizBox. Revisit B85's dismissed path ('Cancelled on the iOS prompt') to match: the same generic Failed with Try Again.
- [x] **F242a** Jailbreak .deb app page: a + bar button where F210's mark toggle was (left of Install/Modify, nav/navbar.js addNavIcon): a tap adds the app to the jailbreak app queue B71a builds (the same job its row swipe / Install queues, browse.js jailbreakAppRowState + rowMenuItems jbApps), quietly with no Confirm sheet, haptic + Undo bar as F193; while queued it shows an x that takes it back out; it follows the queue live (subscribe). F242 already removed the mark toggle and the Mark rows in the jailbreak build (altstore.js handMarking); sideload and site keep the toggle. Check: altstore-marktoggle.test.js covers both builds, tools/check.sh, a 3x render zoomed in (iOS 6 bar-button look, ios6.css approach), then on the 7 via tools/ui-7.sh under its lock. | files: web/app/screens/altstore.js, web/app/screens/altstore-marktoggle.test.js, web/app/ios6.css, web/app/altstore.css | after: B71a
- [x] **B95** (follow-up, critical) Website Install of big IPAs breaks off: the Worker runs out of CPU. User 2026-11-19: Unbound (104 MB, github.com/marioparaschiv/unbound/releases/download/discord-354.0-ios/Unbound.ipa) failed with 'The app's download broke off before the end', several times. Reproduced with curl on ifizbox.com/api/fetch?kind=ipa: 6 of 8 downloads stopped short (175 KB to 99 MB of 104 MB) and ended cleanly instead of with an error. wrangler tail (-c wrangler.ifizbox.toml) shows outcome exceededCpu 'Worker exceeded CPU time limit' on every one. GitHub on its own sends the whole file in under 2 s. Cause: ipaFetch (worker/src/index.js) pipes the body through a JS TransformStream (the byte cap and B87's short-end check), so every chunk costs Worker CPU time. Fix: return r.body untouched (new Response(r.body, ...)), with no JS per chunk, so Cloudflare streams it natively and Content-Length survives. Keep the up-front Content-Length > max refusal. downloadIpa (web/app/signing-client.js) already checks the max and X-IPA-Length on the phone, so it still catches a short body; keep sending X-IPA-Length. Check: deploy, then run curl 10 times on the Unbound URL and get 104175897 bytes every time, with no exceededCpu in wrangler tail. If plain pass-through still goes over the CPU limit, add a resumed download: downloadIpa asks again with Range from byte n and the Worker passes Range through. If neither works, the website must say in words which apps are too big for it (the user wants a hard limit stated). Also fix the leftover home-screen icon: the early install link (F121) leaves a blank, stuck icon on the home screen when signing fails after iOS was sent to the link. Look at whether the failure can tell the Worker to answer iOS's held app.ipa with an error so iOS drops the icon, and otherwise tell the user in the failure text to delete the icon. | files: worker/src/index.js, worker/test.mjs, web/app/signing-client.js, web/app/download-cut.test.js — Also check the other leg, which My IPAs files (picked in Files, kept in IndexedDB) go through too: iOS reading app.ipa from /sign/up/<token>/app.ipa (worker/src/index.js, the ReadableStream pull loop). It copies each 20 MB KV part with data.slice in JS, about 5 copies for a 104 MB app, and could also hit exceededCpu. Test it: sign and upload a 100 MB IPA, curl app.ipa about 5 times, and watch wrangler tail. If it goes over, enqueue the part itself (a Uint8Array view, or KV's stream type) instead of a copy. Website limit to state if one is left: My IPAs keeps files up to 400 MB (SITE_FILE_MAX) and within Safari's storage quota; signing is capped at 512 MB.
- [x] **B93** Selecting text in the install log is very laggy while an install runs, and after Select All the Copy callout took a long time to show (user 2026-11-19, Lite 0.9.9, R19's install). Likely cause: every new log line rewrites or appends to the selectable console text (queue.css .console-out / the progress page's log), so WebKit re-lays out and resets the selection on each line, and progress lines (bytes served) arrive many times a second. Fix: batch log lines into one requestAnimationFrame append at most (never set innerHTML/textContent of the whole log), merge repeated progress lines into the line they update, and while a selection is inside the log (selectionchange) hold new lines in a buffer and append them once the selection ends. Keep it at 120 Hz: no layout reads in the append path. Check: a node test that 200 lines in one frame make one DOM append and that lines are held while a selection exists, a profile in Chromium of a fake install with a selection active (no long tasks over 16 ms), then tools/check.sh. | files: web/app/screens/progress.js, web/app/screens/queue.js, web/app/queue.css, web/app/progress.css, web/app/screens/*.test.js

- [x] **F97** Cydian Account (user 2026-11-18: minimal, free to host): keep your settings, sources, ignored upgrades, installed-app records and signing certificate on cydian.dev, so a new phone, a reinstall or the website gets them back. No email, no password: the account is a random 128-bit **account key** made on the device (shown once as a code and a `cydian://account?k=` link / QR to save or open on another device; kept in the keychain in the app, IndexedDB on the site). Everything is encrypted on the device (AES-GCM, key from the account key with HKDF; Web Crypto in both builds), so cydian.dev only ever holds ciphertext and can't read a certificate. The Worker stores one blob per account in the existing KV namespace (`HOSTED`, a new `acct:` prefix; Cloudflare's free tier: 1 GB, 1,000 writes/day), keyed by SHA-256 of a separate HKDF-derived id, so the key never reaches the server: `PUT /api/account/<id>` (body ≤ 256 KB, an `If-Match` version so two devices don't overwrite each other: the second gets 409 and merges), `GET` (404 when none), `DELETE`. Rate-limit per address, refuse bodies over the cap, no logging of bodies. The payload is `websitePayload()`'s shape (screens/importlink.js: sources, settings, holds, identity) plus core/installed.js's records, versioned (`v: 1`). Settings > Account (iOS 6 grouped table): Create Account / Sign In With Key, Sync Now, Last Synced, Show Account Key, Delete Account (red, asks first); a Sync Certificate switch (on by default, with a footer saying it's encrypted with your key). Signing in is a read. Writes are batched (user 2026-11-18, the free tier's 1,000 writes/day): save once when the app goes to the background or the page is hidden, only if something changed since the last save, plus Sync Now; read on launch. Purchases stay with the repo's own account (Havoc, Chariz), not here. Last-write-wins per field with sources and holds merged as sets. Losing the key loses the data: say so when it's made. t: node tests for encrypt/decrypt round-trip, the id not revealing the key, merge rules and the 409 path against a fake backend; worker tests for the size cap, version check and delete. | files: web/app/core/account.js, web/app/screens/account.js, web/app/screens/settings.js, worker/src/**, native/Sources/** | test: web/app/core/account.test.js, worker tests — User 2026-11-18: approved as drafted. — F102 added a Wish List (localStorage cydian.wishlist, core/wishlist.js): sync it beside holds.

- [x] **B48** App pages: scrolling started an install (user 2026-11-18: 'I was scrolling on an app page and I think menus are too sensitive, it started installing the app when I was just trying to scroll'). Find the path. Suspects: (a) nav/menu.js longPress (500 ms, 10 px) opens a hold menu while the finger rests before a slow scroll, and the lift, or the scroll's touch, then lands on the menu's first item (Install/GET), because the menu takes a pointerup from the same touch that opened it. (b) The page's GET/Install button or a row fires click after a short drag: iOS can still deliver click after small moves, and the scroller (nav/scroller.js) or rowswipe may swallow the pointercancel. Fix it: an item in a menu opened by a hold can't be chosen by the touch that opened it (only by a new tap). A hold or tap is cancelled once the page scrolls (listen for scroll on the scrolling pane, not just pointermove) or on pointercancel. Install/GET and destructive actions ignore a click that ends a gesture that moved more than ~10 px or scrolled. Maybe hold 550-600 ms like iOS. t: node tests with fake pointer/scroll events: a hold then lift doesn't choose an item, a drag that scrolls never clicks Install. One line per phase per app (Downloading Delta 48 MB, Signed, Asked iOS to install, Installed / the reason it failed); percents only on the row's bar and the bottom status. A phase that stops sending progress (0.9.8: Re-sign of Partyboard stuck at 50%) times out into that row's failure, so the queue moves on. — Cancel must really cancel (B66): the signing call is aborted, not left running underneath.
- [x] **F239** (follow-up, low) Queue run (F234): ask about conffiles for rows installed from a shell-fetched .deb. install-deb runs with --force-confold, so the Configuration Upgrade alert (F63) only comes for rows apt downloads and removals (apply-ask). Give install-deb an ask mode (stdin pipe like apply-ask: Apt.swift picks the pipe by the verb) and use it from backend-native.js queueInstall. Check: a test package whose conffile both sides changed asks, on the iPhone 7. | files: native/helper/main.c, native/helper/policy.c, native/helper/paid.c, native/Sources/Apt.swift, web/app/backend-native.js | after: F234
- [x] **F240** (follow-up, normal) Manage's Re-sign (n) / Update (n) (screens/installed.js runManageJobs) on the IPA queue's sign-ahead run (screens/queue.js runAppQueueRun, screens/apprun.js) instead of runAppQueue's one-at-a-time HUD that stops at the first failure; keep B66's 'left picked' for what didn't go through (failed/cancelled rows stay queued). | files: web/app/screens/installed.js, web/app/screens/installed-manage.test.js | after: F235
- [x] **B70** (follow-up, low) Queue run (F234): a shell-fetched .deb is downloaded again by apt. When the file's version and hash match the source's, apt-get install ./copy.deb (install-deb) takes the repo copy and fetches it (seen on the iPhone 7, apt 2.9.4: 'Get:1 http://… f234test-ok'). It's a second download, and a repo that refuses apt (Havoc answers 418) would fail a row the shell fetched fine. Make install-deb install the local file (e.g. an apt option that prefers the local deb, or put the checked copy into apt's archive cache under its expected name before apply). Check: a queue run's Log shows no Get: line for a row marked Downloaded. | files: native/helper/paid.c, native/helper/policy.c, native/helper/main.c, web/app/backend-native.js | after: F234
- [x] **F241** (follow-up, normal) My IPAs' Install through the IPA queue's run: a library job (core/appqueue.js add() takes { library: file } instead of a url; signing-client prepareParams already sends signing.prepare {library}, native SignAhead.swift signs from the library file), queued from the IPA's page so several My IPAs install signed-ahead, one at a time, with per-app stop. Offline (no Signing Server) keeps today's signed-copy message. | files: web/app/core/appqueue.js, web/app/screens/myipas.js, web/app/signing-client.js | after: F235
- [x] **B73** (follow-up, normal) iFizBox's own APT source lists only the newest release, so the jailbreak app's Downgrade (and Version History: '1 version') can't offer older iFizBox builds (user 2026-11-19: 'why does the downgrade not show all releases? fix the source'). Cause: tools/release.sh runs tools/selfrepo.py over only that release's .debs, and each stanza's Filename: is a bare asset name that the Worker's /repo/<file> fetches from releases/latest/download/, so older .debs aren't reachable either. Fix: Packages keeps a stanza per published release (read the earlier releases' Debian-named .debs or their Packages asset; cap like altsource.py's 20), each Filename: a path the Worker maps to that release's asset (e.g. /repo/<tag>/<file> -> releases/download/<tag>/<file>, same SSRF/size rules, no redirect for Zebra), and docs/release.md says so. Check: test_selfrepo.py with two releases, apt on the iPhone 7 sees both versions (apt-cache policy com.ryha.ifizbox), iFizBox's Modify > Downgrade lists them. | files: tools/selfrepo.py, tools/release.sh, tools/test_selfrepo.py, worker/src/**, docs/release.md

Sign an IPA with an extension on the website with the ID kept and install it on a stock phone; if it installs, stop removing them when kept. | files: web/app/websign-job.js | after: F237
- [x] **F243** (follow-up, normal) Keep Bundle IDs on plain Install and My IPAs: before a plain Install (signing-client.js signAndInstall, no Custom Install sheet) warn once when the original app (same bundle ID, not signed by iFizBox: core/keepid.js originalState) is on the phone, since iOS refuses a signed app over the App Store copy; add the Keep Bundle ID switch to My IPAs' Sign Options sheet (library.options keepBundleId) and make My IPAs' offline Sign follow the global setting (Library.sign uses only the file's options today). | files: web/app/signing-client.js, web/app/screens/myipas.js, native/sign/Library.swift, native/Sources/MyIPAs.swift | after: F237

Ask it for a fresh install (no record) before the job is queued or when Confirm runs, and turn 'Install a Copy' into the job's keepBundleId: false. | files: web/app/screens/altstore.js, web/app/screens/queue.js | after: F243
- [x] **F249** (follow-up, low) Original-app warning on a list row's swipe and hold-menu Install (sideload app): browse.js queues rowAppJob's job straight in, so F243's once-per-app question (signing-client.js originalWarning) is never asked there. - [x] **B84** (follow-up, normal) In the jailbreak app, installing iFizBox's own .deb from inside iFizBox (seen with Modify > Downgrade to 0.9.8-1557 on the iPhone 7; the Upgrade banner / Changes upgrade of com.ryha.ifizbox very likely goes the same way) leaves dpkg with com.ryha.ifizbox 'half-configured': apt's term.log for that run never ends, and every later apt-get (iFizBox's own installs included) stops with 'dpkg was interrupted, you must manually run dpkg --configure -a'. Likely cause: the helper's apt-get runs as a child of the app, and postinst's 'uicache -p' on the running app makes iOS close the app, which takes apt, dpkg and postinst down with it. Fix ideas: run a self-install's apt detached from the app (setsid/launchd job, or the upgrades daemon), or skip/defer uicache when the app is the caller, and have the helper run 'dpkg --configure -a' when it finds an interrupted dpkg. Check on the 7 afterwards: dpkg -s com.ryha.ifizbox shows 'install ok installed' after an in-app downgrade and upgrade. Details: journal/2026-11-19-r28-downgrade-older-builds. | files: native/helper/**, native/deb/postinst, native/Sources/Apt.swift
- [x] **B88** (follow-up, low) Jailbreak queue run (F234, one package at a time) with iFizBox's own package (com.ryha.ifizbox) and others: nothing orders iFizBox last, so once its dpkg run puts the new Info.plist on disk the app's Replaced watcher (App.swift, 3 s timer) closes the app and the packages queued after it never run (likely; not tried on the phone; found in B84, which made the apt run itself survive the app closing). Fix idea: core/installrun.js puts iFizBox's own row last in the dpkg lane (after everything that doesn't depend on it), and/or Replaced holds its close while a helper run is going. Check on the 7: queue iFizBox + a small package, both install. | files: web/app/core/installrun.js, web/app/core/installrun.test.js
- [x] **F253** (follow-up, low) UDID from a configuration profile on the website: the Worker serves a Profile Service .mobileconfig (PayloadType Profile Service, DeviceAttributes UDID/PRODUCT/VERSION, a per-visit Challenge, URL = an https /udid endpoint); iOS POSTs a CMS-signed plist, the Worker reads the plist out of it (find <?xml ... </plist>, no crypto), keeps {udid, product, version} in KV under the challenge for the TTL, and answers 301 to a page that says to go back. The website (Safari or the Home Screen web app, which don't share storage, so it polls the Worker by challenge) then fills This iPhone in Settings > Signing, which today says unknown for any profile with a device list. Unsigned profile shows Not Verified: say so on the page. Background: journal/2026-11-19-r30-free-apple-id/notes.md section 4. | files: worker/src/udid.js, worker/src/index.js, worker/test.mjs, web/app/screens/signing.js, web/app/screens/settings.js, docs/signing.md
- [x] **D8** (follow-up, low) docs/signing.md: describe the website download's Range resume (downloadIpa, CUT_TRIES; /api/fetch?kind=ipa passes Range bytes=n- and the body through untouched) and POST /sign/upload/<token>/fail (early link dropped when signing fails; the failure text says to delete a blank icon). | files: docs/signing.md
- [x] **F256** (follow-up, low) Website installs use the UDID F253's profile found: signing-client.js siteCause passes storedUdid() (screens/signing.js) to deviceCheck instead of null, so an install whose phone isn't in the profile says so with the UDID (and could refuse before signing, like device_not_in_profile in the apps), and one that is in it drops the likely-cause line | files: web/app/signing-client.js, web/app/signing-client-web.test.js | after: F253
- [x] **B96** (follow-up, low) Range reads of /sign/up/<token>/app.ipa cost ~37 ms Worker CPU (seen in wrangler tail, 2026-11-19): a Range's cut first/last part is read whole as arrayBuffer (20 MB each) in upPump. Read only what's needed (KV stream, skip natively or cancel early) so iOS's Range requests stay under the CPU limit. | files: worker/src/index.js, worker/test.mjs
- [x] **F255** (follow-up, low) Jailbreak build: Changes' Upgrade (N) queues its app (IPA) upgrades into the app queue (app-queue.js) beside apt's packages, so one Confirm runs them all (screens/queue.js runJailbreakApps: the helper's install-ipa on the black page with rows), instead of B68's one openIpaInstall console per app. Also consider the not-installed app page's Install queueing the same way (today it installs at once, B69). Check on the 7 with an app upgrade in Changes. | files: web/app/screens/changes.js, web/app/screens/browse.js, web/app/screens/*.test.js | after: B71a
- [x] **R33** (follow-up, normal) Free route shape check (after the scratch LiveContainer test site, see F254 notes): (1) can iFizBox Lite run as a guest app inside LiveContainer (signed by the same free certificate), and from inside it reinstall/renew LiveContainer itself through installation_proxy (idevice + pairing file + LocalDevVPN), the way LiveContainer+SideStore does? That gives 1 slot used, 2 free. (2) iOS 27: can the phone make its own pairing file (network pairing with a PIN under Developer Mode, cf. StikPair/PhonePair: check licenses) so no computer is needed at all? (3) Cheap check: on a phone running Tailscale, does a connection from an app to the phone's own Tailscale IP reach lockdownd (so Tailscale users needn't switch to LocalDevVPN, iOS allows one VPN at a time)? data (2026-11-19, from the desktop): lockdownd on the iPhone 15 answers QueryType over the LAN (192.168.0.41:62078 -> Type com.apple.mobile.lockdown), but via its Tailscale IP 100.119.220.13:62078 the TCP connect is accepted then reset at once, every time (port 1 is refused, so something on 62078 is reached and drops it). Remote-over-Tailscale lockdown: no. Still to test phone-to-self from an app on the phone (iSH: nc -v 100.119.220.13 62078 vs own Wi-Fi IP vs 10.7.0.1 with LocalDevVPN): held open = accepted, closes at once = rejected.

The user runs steps (a)-(c) in journal/2026-11-20-r-free-route-stock-check/notes.md and records the answers; (b) decides go/no-go. — RESULT (2026-11-19, iPhone 15 Pro iOS 27.0.1, free Apple ID via the scratch test site, syslog over USB): itms-services install of a free-account-signed app is refused by installd: MIInstallerErrorDomain Code=111 'The bundle being installed ... is authorized by a free provisioning profile, but apps validated by those are not allowed to be installed from this source.' (LegacyErrorString ApplicationVerificationFailed; shown as 'Unable to Install, please try again later'; with get-task-allow false it shows 'integrity could not be verified'). The SAME signed IPA installs fine over USB via installation_proxy (ideviceinstaller), free profile accepted. Same for our test app and Feather 2.9.0. So free-account apps can't be installed by the website/install prompt at all: the free route needs installation_proxy (idevice + pairing file + LocalDevVPN, F15) inside the app. Question (b) update-in-place over itms is moot. Sign-in/devportal all worked (v3 anisette; v1 shared anisette got -36607 and the account locked once).
- [x] **F261** (follow-up, normal) Set the website's signing cap from the measured ceiling (F257 measured it; IPA_MAX core/websign.js = SITE_FILE_MAX core/myipas.js = MAX_UPLOAD_MB stay 512 MB until a person picks the number). iPhone 7 Safari, real zsign WebAssembly, stored (incompressible) test IPAs, fresh tab: 42 MB and 70 MB sign and upload; 100 MB signed 1 time in 3 (the other two: 'zsign stopped: Out of memory', no tab reload; the phone had ~17 MB free pages). So the 7's ceiling is 70-100 MB of unpacked app, while Unbound is 104 MB compressed (more unpacked) and an iPhone 15 Pro (8 GB) can do far more: one global number either blocks newer phones or lets the 7 fail. Decide: a cap per device (navigator.deviceMemory isn't in Safari; guess from the screen/UA model, or learn it: remember the size that failed with Out of memory on this browser and offer the Too Big alert above it), or a lower global cap, or a zsign that streams instead of holding the IPA, the unpacked app and the output at once. Journal: journal/2026-11-19-f257-big-apps. | files: web/app/core/websign.js, web/app/core/myipas.js, web/app/signing-client.js, worker/wrangler.toml, worker/wrangler.ifizbox.toml
- [x] **R34** (follow-up, low) Website IPA install: find a signal that iOS's Install prompt closed. On iOS 15.8.8 (iPhone 7) neither Safari nor a home-screen web app fires focus/blur/visibilitychange/pageshow when the itms-services prompt opens or is cancelled, and document.hasFocus() stays true (B90 journal), so B90's watchPrompt only catches a cancel at its 30 s cap. Check iOS 17+/26 with the same probe page, and look for other signals (requestAnimationFrame throttled under the alert, timer drift). If one works, shorten the cancel detection in web/app/signing-client.js watchPrompt. | files: web/app/signing-client.js

### Native jailbreak app (shape decided: docs/native.md)
- [x] **T3** Cydian's own iOS SDK setup, so the build depends on no other project: tools/ios-sdk.sh of our own design that extracts iPhoneOS.sdk from an Xcode .xip on Linux (xar/pbzx or equivalent) and fixes what lld needs (arm64e slices in .tbd stubs, per docs/native.md '2. Building on Linux'). The native build finds the SDK only through $IOS_SDK and says how to get one when it's missing; no default path into another project's folders. t: the script's steps on a small fixture archive; the build fails with a clear message when $IOS_SDK is unset. | files: tools/ios-sdk.sh, tools/test_ios_sdk.py
- [x] **N1** Swift shell in `native/` that builds `Cydian.deb` (rootless, `iphoneos-arm64`, arm64 + arm64e, deployment target iOS 15) on Linux: an app showing `web/app/` from its bundle in a WKWebView, `CADisableMinimumFrameDurationOnPhone`, the navigation approach R3 picked, version number and icon. Add the build to tools/check.sh when the toolchain is present. t: `make package`; the user installs it on a modern jailbroken phone and the journal records frame rate during scrolling and swipe-back. | files: native/**, tools/check.sh | after: R3, T1 — User 2026-11-17: no frame-rate measurement needed; the user installs it and reports if anything feels off. — User 2026-11-17: no newer jailbroken phone yet, so nothing gets installed on a device. Done = the .deb builds and its contents check out (control file, rootless paths, the app bundle with web/app/ inside); never block on a device test. — R3: navigation = spike A (one WKWebView, JS stack); switch off WebKit's PreferPageRenderingUpdatesNear60FPSEnabled (private _WKFeature). Build arm64 only with native/spike/build.sh's recipe (swift.org 6.4.2 + iOS 27.1 SDK + rustup ld64.lld; arm64e won't link and isn't needed). docs/native.md 'R3 answers'.
- [x] **N2** Root access the way R3 found Sileo and Zebra do it on iOS 13+ (docs/native.md "3. Root"): no setuid binary; spawn apt as root with the persona spawn attributes, the app signed with `com.apple.private.persona-mgmt` and the other entitlements listed there. One small module that runs only `apt-get update|install|remove|upgrade` and `dpkg --configure -a` (from `/var/jb` on rootless), with an allowlist of arguments (package names matching `[a-z0-9.+-]+`, no options from the caller). It checks the caller is Cydian and streams output back. t: unit tests on Linux for the argument checks; journal of a real run on a modern jailbroken phone. | files: native/helper/** | after: N1 — User 2026-11-17: no jailbroken test phone. Verify with the Linux unit tests only; skip the real-device run and say in the journal it is untested on a device. — R3: on iOS 13+ Sileo and Zebra don't use a setuid helper; they posix_spawn as root with posix_spawnattr_set_persona_np(99, OVERRIDE) + persona uid/gid 0, allowed by the app's com.apple.private.persona-mgmt entitlement. Build N2's helper as a non-setuid allowlist binary the app spawns that way; caller check = uid 0 + parent executable same dev/inode as Cydian. Details: docs/native.md 'Root'.
- [x] **N3** `web/app/backend-native.js` plus the Swift side of the bridge: sources from apt's sources files, raw lists from apt's lists directory after `apt-get update` through the helper, dpkg status, all fed into the same core as the site. Every screen from F4-F7 then works in the app unchanged. | files: web/app/backend-native.js, native/** | after: N2, F7 — User 2026-11-17: no jailbroken test phone. Test the bridge against a fake helper (fixture apt lists and dpkg status) so it runs without a device. — N2 landed native/helper/ (cydian-helper + spawn.c's cydian_helper_spawn, see native/helper/README.md). Not wired yet: build it in native/build.sh (make -C helper ios, or inline), ship at /var/jb/usr/libexec/cydian/cydian-helper (mode 0755, no setuid) and have mkdeb.py check it, add com.apple.private.persona-mgmt to native/entitlements.plist, call cydian_helper_spawn from Swift (bridging header) and read fds 1/2/3. N4's apt-get -s is not in the allowlist: run it unprivileged. — F22: the web side expects the bridge to expose cydianNative.helper(words: string[]) -> Promise<{ code, stdout, stderr }> that runs cydian-helper with those words (web/app/screens/settings.js passes it to createSettings; core/settings.js holds.set/sync call ["hold"|"unhold", pkg] and ["showhold"]). The helper's allowlist now has hold/unhold <installed pkg> and showhold (apt-mark). — Bridge method haptic.tap (web/app/nav/haptic.js already sends it when globalThis.cydianApp is set): a light UIImpactFeedbackGenerator tick, used when the scroll indicator is grabbed. — F7: web/app/catalog.js reads the installed list from cydianNative.dpkgStatus() when it exists: a function resolving to the text of /var/lib/dpkg/status or /var/jb/var/lib/dpkg/status (core/installed.js STATUS_PATHS), or null. After an install/remove call catalog.reloadInstalled().
